September 11, 2024 09:00 PM GMT

China Property Management & Services | Asia Pacific

# 1H24 Core Business Resilient; Capital Return Protected Downside

Despite sluggish VAS growth amid weak macro, PMS core business held up well in 1H at mid-teens growth. With a lower base into 2H, we expect 7% earnings growth in 2024E with 6% dividend yield. Prefer CR Mixc, GTS and PPS.

Weak 1H24 results but leading players showed resilient profit on enhanced operating efficiencies: Core profit +4% y-y on average (+17% y-y for SOEs, -18% y-y for POEs). Overall revenue growth slowed down on weaker VAS growth, but leading players' PMS business posted mid-teens growth with stable margin. Developer-related risks lingered incl. special impairments and increasing A/R from parent developers, DVAS earnings drags even for SOEs, and margin compression on projects with high vacancy rates. However, further downside likely manageable given lower non-recurring business earnings contribution and better corporate governance after 2 years of earnings reset.

Cashflow and op. efficiencies key priorities in 2H: 1H OCF deteriorated with weaker cash collection from res. property owners. Non-res. projects were stable on selective project expansions and cutting low-profit projects. Better service quality and client portfolio optimization key to improving cashflow in 2H. With greater emphasis on op. efficiency amid weak macro, we prefer PMCs with early mover advantages in tech and strong cost control.

Third-party competition heightened; prefer mall operators for strong cashflow and accelerating market consolidation: 1H third-party expansion -13% on rising competition and tighter budget for govt and corp clients. Given shrinking primary market, we expect PMCs with expertise in secondary and non-res markets to outperform. Within PMCs, we prefer shopping mall operators for: 1) stronger cashflow thanks to higher cash collection rate; and 2) market share gain for leading players amid market downturn, leveraging strong op. capabilities and brand equity to attract tenants (e.g. CR Mixc and CWTC).

We expect 7% profit growth in 2024E and 6% div. yield + improving capital return protecting downside: Given current macro headwinds, we prefer defensive companies with sustainable organic growth, strong cashflow and potential div. upside. We downgrade Greentown Management from OW to EW on uncertain div. outlook constrained by poor cashflow. We prefer CR Mixc, GTS and PPS on higher visibility and potential capital return upside.

Cara Zhu assumes coverage of A-living, Country Garden Services, Sunac Services and China World Trade Center Co. with this report.

## Investment Thesis

## 1H24 results weak on lackluster VAS but PMS core business still resilient at +14% y-y on average; wider divergence between SOEs and POEs

Property management companies (PMC) reported weak 1H24 results, with core profit +4% y-y on average. SOEs posted solid results of +17% growth vs. POEs declining -18% y-y with a lingered negative impact from related-party decoupling.

1) Revenue growth slowed to 9% y-y from 12% in 1H23 (+10% for SOEs and +3% for POEs) ( Exhibit 8 ) but the revenue itself was more optimally structured with 73% from PMS (vs. 69% in 1H23). On the flip side, CVAS was stagnant on weak consumption while propertyrelated non-recurring business (esp. DVAS, property agent and carpark sales) remained the main drags.

Segment-wise, basic property management services (PMS) still recorded steady growth of +14% y-y; CVAS rose 2% y-y with milder growth for both SOEs and POEs amid consumption headwinds; DVAS remained the main drag with a 25% decline on average (- 9% for SOEs and -35% for POEs) ( Exhibit 11 , Exhibit 12 , Exhibit 13 ). Despite the continued slowdown for POEs, SOEs' DVAS businesses also turned negative against the backdrop of the prolonged property downturn. However, we do not expect SOEs' DVAS business to shrink as dramatically as POEs, as their parent SOE developers' financial situations remain solid with limited liquidity concerns. In 1H24, the revenue contribution from DVAS had already fallen to 5% (vs. 8% in 1H23), split 13% for SOEs and 3% for POEs.

Overall, the steady growth of PMS demonstrates its defensive business nature amid the macro downturn, while the growth of the B2B business (CVAS, DVAS and PFM, etc.) may be a swing factor and be highly dependent on the overall macro environment.

2) Gross margin stable overall with a narrower decline after the sharp drop in 2022 and 2023 ( Exhibit 9 ) where GM fell -1ppt to 19.8% on average (SOE +0.3 ppt to 23.7%, POEs - 1.5ppts to 19.3%). SOE GPM continued to expand on improving operating efficiencies and better cost control, while POEs' GPM declined on: 1) lower revenue contribution from high-margin businesses (i.e. CVAS and DVAS); and 2) higher portion of vacancy property management fees and increasing contribution from third-party projects, which dragged down the overall gross margin. However, for companies half-way through earnings resets (e.g. ALIV, CGS and SUNS), the margin decline narrowed from -3.8ppts in 1H23 to -2.2ppts, due mainly to: 1) stabilizing revenue mix with the focus back on core business and less disruption from DVAS and non-recurring business; and 2) normalizing segment margins after adopting a more prudent accounting policy. Thus, we expect relatively stable margin trends ahead.

3) SG&A ratio down a further 0.6ppt on cost savings, with -1.1ppts on average for SOEs and 0.6ppt on average for POEs. This indicates SOEs may have more room to improve operating efficiencies and sustain their bottom-line growth amid macro headwinds ( Exhibit 10 ).

3) Related-party impairment lingered, but impact was almost non-cash ( Exhibit 14 ); Aliving and Sunac Services still recorded related-party receivable impairment in 1H24.

Greentown Service (GTS) also posted a Rmb75mn special impairment (after-tax) for historical investments. Onewo recorded Rmb236mn of amortization for previous M&As.

Looking into 2H24, further impairment risks still exist for CGS (goodwill and loan to thirdparty), ALIV (related-party receivables) and Onewo (related-party receivables).

For PMCs with special impairment/amortization (A-Living, CGS, Onewo, Sunac Services), all except A-Living adjusted their dividend base to core profit (adding back special impairment and amortization). Thus, although the impairment risk may still linger for POEs, the impact should not affect dividends and cash flow, in our view.

4) Dividends raised: CR Mixc, COPH and Onewo raised their 1H24 dividend payout ratio to improve shareholder return.

5) Cash collection and OCF pressure from third parties need more attention. Most PMCs recorded negative OCF in 1H24, partially on seasonality, though most admitted rising difficulties collecting management fees from residential projects and delayed payments amid the current weak macro environment. Among our covered PMCs, only Mixc posted positive OCF growth in 1H, indicating strong cashflow from the shopping mall management business.

Transition underway: DVAS drag diminishing, but CVAS need more time to regain momentum; cash collection on third-party projects and operating efficiencies are two priorities in 2H.

## Non-recurring business drag lingered even for SOEs, but impact is manageable; CVAS recovery slowed down amid weak consumption

Non-recurring business, including DVAS, property agency, carpark sales and home decoration business, accounted for 10% of total revenue (vs. 13% in 1H23) and 12% of gross profit in 1H24 (vs. 16% in 1H23), including 7% from DVAS and 5% from non-recurring CVAS. While POEs still experienced sharp y-y decline of -34% y-y on average in terms of non-recurring gross profit, some PMCs saw h-h improvement after the deep reset in 2H23, such as CGS, ALIV and SUNS, indicating limited further downside risks from developerrelated business. In contrast, SOEs saw their first y-y decline (-6%) for non-recurring business in 1H24 amid the worsening property market.

Admittedly, earnings drag from non-recurring business may put pressure on SOE growth over the next few years, but we believe the overall impact is manageable, as: 1) relateddevelopers' SOE backgrounds and solid balance sheets could protect against liquidity downside risk as POEs and ensure PMC cash-flow stability; 2) market share gain from parent developers may partially offset market shortfall; and 3) SOEs' more resilient PMS growth may help alleviate the earnings drag from non-recurring business during property decoupling.

We forecast non-recurring business to account for only 7% of revenue and 8% of gross profit by 2024E, 11% for SOEs (vs. 16% in 2023) and 6% for POEs (vs. 13% in 2023).

Meanwhile, growth for CVAS also slowed down with +2% y-y revenue growth and +2% y-y gross profit growth on average in 1H24, with no clear signs of turnaround. Divergence also showed, with +16% y-y growth on average for SOEs and -14% y-y for POEs amid weak consumption and PMCs' focus on core business for better cashflow.

Despite the earnings drag from property-related CVAS (e.g. home decoration, property agent and carpark sales), recurring CVAS (e.g. community retails, home-living services, advertising) also slowed down owing to consumption headwinds. Gross profit growth decelerated from 14% in 2023 to 8% in 1H24. Divergence also showed, with +17% y-y growth on average for SOEs and +2% y-y for POEs, partially due to POEs refocusing on core business lines to preserve cashflow.

Given the uncertain macro outlook, we expect -1%/+2%/6% revenue growth and 0%/- 1%/4% gross profit growth for the CVAS segment in 2024-26E, respectively, with lowteens growth for POEs in 24-25E before gradually bottoming out in 2026E.

Note: Non-recurring CVAS include property agency, carpark sales, furnishing and decoration, tourism, and tech hardware installments. Source: Company data, Morgan Stanley Research (E) estimates

## Third-party cash collection still the key focus in 2H

1H24 cash collection fell 3-5ppts for most companies, with rising difficulties on all fronts, esp. from residential property owners. While the current-period fee collection ratio was relatively stable thanks to more proactive cash collection methods, most companies found it more difficult collecting delayed payments and advance payments, indicating that project owners were reluctant to spend amid the wider economic downturn. Thus, better service quality and selective project expansion are key to improving the cash collection ratio, in our view.

In addition to weaker cash collection from the PMS business, property-related VAS cashflow also came under pressure. The A/R balance increased 19% y-y in 1H24, with A/R days extended to 125 on average. SOEs, including Mixc, PPS and COPH, still had solid balance sheets with A/R days of 51-77 (still a reasonable level) while POE A/R days were much longer, partially arising from longer payment times from related developers and B/G customers due to POEs' weaker bargaining power. Notably, Onewo has seen a significant A/R increase and longer A/R days due to delayed payments from Vanke; this has deteriorated its current OCF and may trigger higher impairment risks.

Overall cash balance -11% y-y in 1H24 vs. +9% y-y in 1H23 and +15% y-y in 2023, in our view. Given the defensive and asset-light nature of PMCs, stable cashflow is the most essential metric. While most companies are still targeting full-year OCF >1x core profit, we think this could be challenging for some POEs to achieve (e.g. CGS and ALIV).

## Impairment risks lingering but impact is non-cash

For potential impairments from A/R and goodwill, POEs have made 50% provisions for related-party account receivables, and we think further impairments are likely given the difficulties collecting fees from related developers.

• As of 1H24, A/R from related parties stood at Rmb785mn on average, accounting for 16% for total A/R, and 0.5x 2024E core profit (POE average of 1.2x and SOE average of 0.4x) ( Exhibit 26 )

• The cumulative impairment ratio of total A/R was 13% for POEs and 4% for SOEs, and the cumulative impairment ratio of related-party A/R was 74% for POEs. CGS, ALIV and Sunac Services have made higher provisions for related-party A/R with 74-80% impairment ratios, indicating less impairment drag in the future ( Exhibit 24 ). However, we see potential downside risks for Onewo as it has made no additional impairments for related-party AR so far, while the A/R balance from its parentco surged in 1H24, indicating a low cash collection rate.

• No special goodwill impairment in 1H24, but likely to be for full year, with potential risks for ALIV, CGS and SUNS.

However, as most companies with special impairments have changed their dividend policy to pay dividends based on core earnings (adding back special impairments and amortization), further impairments should have no material impact on dividends and cash flow, in our view. Meanwhile, incremental A/R from related parties is key to watch.

## Non-residential and secondary markets likely to be next battlefield for third-party expansion; SOEs and high quality players continue to outshine

With more focus on core business as PMCs decouple from property developers, thirdparty expansion has become the key growth driver for PMCs. Growth in this area softened in 2023 and 1H24 amid rising competition and weak macro. The divergence between SOEs and POEs is widening ( Exhibit 27 ).

• Overall third-party expansion slowed down in 1H, with 13% y-y contract value decline to an average of Rmb1bn, or 44% of 2023 contract value, due to heightened competition amid weak macro and PMCs' more selective project expansion as they align their shifting focus from scale to cash flow stability.

• The overall contract value of Rmb1bn on average was still at an industry-leading level. The annualized amount was equivalent to 21% of 2023 PMS revenue, sufficient to support mid-teen growth for PMS business in future.

• SOEs (including GTS and Onewo) are generally on track to achieve their expansion targets, thanks to diversified project portfolios and resources from parent developers to penetrate public projects. CR Mixc and COPH have already completed c.50% of their full-year target, while PPS was slightly behind due to more prudent criteria for public projects for the sake of better profitability. Semi-SOEs GTS and Onewo were up 3%/24% y-y with overall value at an industryleading level.

• POEs recorded 17% y-y declines in third-party expansion on average, due to intensifying competition and damaged brand names. A-Living, CGS and Sunac Services were down 21%/9%/20%, respectively, in 1H24.

SOEs have a competitive advantage in third-party expansion given their stronger brand names and relationships with government as well as superior expertise and experience compared to POEs.

For POEs, we think the second stage of independent growth is yet to be proven, given: 1) The POE brand has been damaged due to the default of their related developers since 2021; and 2) the more competitive market, with a declining primary residential market size, excessive competition in secondary residential expansion, and SOEs' growing presence in non-residential expansion.

We forecast a 13% CAGR for GFA under management in 2023-26E, 9% CAGR for SOEs and 14% for SOEs.

We expect an earnings turnaround in 2H24, with 7% growth in 2024E and 6% dividend yield; we forecast 10% CAGR in 2023-26E and improving capital management

After over two years of earnings decline, we expect an earnings inflection to become evident at the 2024 results, with average earnings growth of 7%/10%/9% in 2024E/25E/26E, respectively ( Exhibit 31 ), or a 10% CAGR over 2023-26E (14% for SOEs and 2% for POEs) on a 9% revenue CAGR, relative stable gross margin and lower SG&A ratio.

• PMS will be the key growth driver, with 13% CAGR revenue growth, on a 13% CAGR for GFA under management in 2023-26E: 1) Related developers contributed 11-23mn sqm p.a. on average; and 2) Third parties contributed 45-55mn sqm p.a. on average. We estimate GFA delivered from related developers will contribute to 19% of new GFA in 2026E (vs. 26% in 2023).

• Community VAS to see moderate growth of 4% revenue CAGR amid lackluster macro backdrop.

• DVAS could still drag on revenue with a 20% CAGR decrease, -5% for SOEs and - 37% for POEs. But revenue contribution will further diminish to 2% in 2026E from 7% in 2023.

To enhance capital management efficiency, PMCs have actively adapted the following methods: 1) Share buybacks ( Exhibit 34 ): POEs cumulatively spent HK\$275mn on share buybacks 2024 YTD (or 0.8% of total shares on average) vs. HK\$486mn in 2023 (or 0.9% of total shares). Among them, GTS and Onewo did the highest amount at 0.8% of total shares. Onewo resumed share buybacks after its 1H results and also raised its dividends. We believe CR Mixc and PPS could raise their dividend payout ratios in 2024E also.

Looking ahead, we expect PMCs to raise dividends further and conduct consecutive share buybacks to improve capital management and ROE.

# Changes to Key Stocks, Ratings and Price Targets

The industry is trading at 8.2x 2025E P/E, with a 10% earnings CAGR in 2023-26E (13% for SOEs and 11% for POEs). We believe current valuations reflect the moderating growth outlook and potential lingering impact from the worsening property market situation and macro headwinds.

However, the market may undervalue the longevity of PMCs' defensive business models, strong net cash position and stable cashflow, which could bring further upside by way of improving capital return (dividend hike + share buyback).

We lower our PTs by 14% on average owing to slower earnings growth outlook (-8% average) and a lower 2025x P/E (from 9x to 8x on average) to reflect the challenging growth outlook and investors' lower risk appetite. Our preferred names remain CR Mixc, GTS and PPS for their more visible growth profile and potential capital return upside backed by healthy cash flow.

We downgrade Greentown Management to EW from OW and lower our PT to HK\$3.18 on 6x 2024E P/E vs. 12x before.

## Key Stocks

CR Mixc (1209.HK, PT HK\$33.23, 36% upside): We like CR Mixc for its: 1) well-rounded 1H results with significant dividend bonus, implying 6.5% dividend yield for the full year; 2) strong position to deliver 20%+ earnings growth in 2024-25E with accelerating market consolidation and a strong pipeline; and 3) its leading position among shopping mall players with sustainable organic growth ability and strong cashflow, which adds further dividend upside.

Greentown Service (2869.HK, PT HK\$4.42, 20% upside): We like GTS for its: 1) solid 1H24 results on better-than-expected margin improvement, which reflects strong management execution; 2) raised 2024 guidance (from 15% to 20% y-y core operating profit growth) with now one of the highest growth estimates among our covered PMCs, well supported by further room for margin expansion off a low base; and 3) newly launched option scheme, which better aligns employee interests.

Poly Property Services (6049.HK, PT HK\$32.41, 25% upside): We like Poly Property Services for its: 1) strong parent developer with an SOE background, consolidating the market; 2) unrivaled competitive edge and strong expansion track record in public projects and ramping-up of its secondary residential and office building expansion, which should partially offset any shortfall in primary residential space; 3) well-managed cash collection, especially from public projects, thanks to selective project expansion and proactive cash collection; and 4) deep valuation of 1.6x/1.5x of 2024/25E ex-cash P/E (vs. industry average of 4.2/3.7x for 2024/2025E), backed by its strong net cash position of c.60% of market cap – one of the lowest valuations among our covered PMCs.

## Rating changes

We downgrade Greentown Management to EW from OW and lower our PT to HK\$3.18 from HK\$7.58 on our new 2024E P/E of 6x (vs. 12x before), with 2% earnings CAGR in 2024-26E. We cut 2024E/2025E/2026E earnings by 18%/28%/34%, respectively, to

Rmb1,191mn/Rmb1,409mn/Rmb1,653mn, to reflect: 1) disappointing 1H24 results; 2) increasing cash collection pressure and muted construction and new starts amid current weak macro situation; and 3) uncertain dividend policy ahead constrained by weak cashflow. We downgrade from OW to EW to reflect: 1) uncertain earnings outlook; 2) low dividend visibility; and 3) key management changes, which raise concerns regarding execution. However, we think the current share price has already largely priced in this rather lukewarm outlook. We could turn more constructive once the company has more earnings visibility and a clear dividend policy. Move to EW.

## PT and earnings estimate revisions

On average, we lower our 2024/2025/2026 earnings estimates by 6%/8%/8% to reflect: 1) heightened competition on third-party expansion; 2) more growth and margin pressure on CVAS as a result of slower-than-expected consumption recovery with most companies potentially seeing flat or low-single-digit growth in 2024-25E; 3) further slowdown in DVAS potentially bringing about limited margin downside pressure for PMC companies and even for SOEs. On the positive side, we think operating efficiency enhancements may partially offset top-line pressures.

For our price target changes, we apply 8x 2025E P/E on average (12x for SOEs and 7x for POEs) to reflect the moderate earnings outlook and weak market sentiment.

## HK\$1.85PRICE TARGET

HK\$1.85: Base case, applying a 3.0x target P/E to our 2025 EPS estimate. We arrive at our target P/E multiple based on our scorecard for China's property management companies, which is based on seven metrics: GFA growth pro <sup>fi</sup>le, parent developer, <sup>fi</sup>nancials and cash <sup>fl</sup>ows, revenue quality, service quality, pro <sup>fi</sup>tability, and incentive scheme.

## UNDERWEIGHT THESIS

▪ We expect a 2023-26E earnings CAGR of -12%.
▪ Margin compression in CVAS and DVAS may continue in the following few years. ▪ DVAS segment may further shrink amid weak property sales.

![](images/720e7db14a65d67d670ceeb08f5dcae2f0b22f02023bd067c84ba8957a023249.jpg)

## BULL CASE

## 7.0x 2025E P/E

## HK\$4.32 BASE CASE

Accelerating GFA growth with better-thanexpected margin: Higher-than-expected growth in GFA under management, driven by faster-than-expected third-party GFA expansion. Higher-than-expected property management fee increases as property owners value high-quality service more, which ultimately leads to faster-thanexpected margin expansion. Increasing interest in the industry from more IPOs and rising social and government attention, driving valuation upward.

## 3.0x 2025E P/E

## HK\$1.85

Stable GFA growth with steady ASP and normalized margin: Stable growth in GFA under management, backed by on-track third-party GFA expansion. We expect relatively stable property management fees with no signi cant increases in the near term.

## BEAR CASE

## HK\$1.23

## 2.0x 2025E P/E

Slower-than-expected GFA growth with margin squeeze: Slower-than-expected growth in GFA under management, hampered by a slower delivery schedule from Agile and Greenland, along with weaker-than-expected third-party GFA expansion. Tightening regulation on management fees and slower-than-expected VAS development further cap earnings upside.

![](images/a9e58b63154ccd89bb99d4e886c6c237caa1c5d5f7e73386fc3429f5cb6c3437.jpg)

## OVERWEIGHT THESIS

▪ CR Mixc is a leader in the shopping mall market and bene ts from a strong pipeline. ▪ Residential property management of CR Mixc bene ts from a strong parent
developer, and its expertise and good
reputation in third-party projects expansion. ▪ We like CR Mixc for its commercial
property management exposure, strong shopping mall operational capability and steady growth outlook. Non-luxury brands are the key growth driver.
▪ Valuation is attractive at 14x forward P/E (0.6x PEG), with 6.5% dividend yield for 2024.

▪ After upbeat special dividend in 1H results, we expect further dividend upside given rich cash position and strong operating cash ow.

Vanke Overhang Lingers

## HK\$19.39PRICE TARGET

Base case, derived from 10x our 2025 EPS estimate of Rmb1.76. Our target P/E multiple is based on our scorecard for China's property management companies, which is based on: GFA scale (with a 10/10 score), growth channels (5/5), project type (4/5), parent developer (15/20), <sup>fi</sup>nancials & cash <sup>fl</sup>ows (16/20), revenue contribution from CVAS (4/5) and revenue reliance (7/10), service quality (5/5), pro <sup>fi</sup>tability (6/10), incentive (7/10).

## EQUAL-WEIGHT THESIS

▪ Industry-leading scale with proven thirdparty expansion ability and on-track margin expansion under the Onewo Town Strategy. ▪ Solid mid-teen growth for core business, but earnings drag from developer-related business weighs on overall growth.
▪ Increasing related-party A/R deteriorating operating cash ow.
▪ Headline risks from Vanke may weigh on sentiment toward Onewo.

![](images/28d4c33c0b5a6501dda579acf494ceb793df5a4ba95da67e8d4bb08a7dd22a03.jpg)
Source: Re nitiv, Morgan Stanley Research
Source: Re nitiv, Morgan Stanley Research

## BULL CASE

## 15x 2025E P/E

## HK\$29.08 BASE CASE

Accelerating GFA growth, better-thanexpected margin, and concerns on Vanke's liquidity removed: We assume 1) higher GFA under management growth, driven by stronger recovery in the parentco's sales, faster third-party GFA expansion and more M&A contribution; 2) higher increases in property management fees that lead to faster margin expansion; 3) better growth in community VAS and technology services; and 4) concerns on Vanke's liquidity issue removed.

## 10x 2025E P/E

## HK\$19.39

Stable GFA growth with steady ASP, normalized margin, less support from parentco: We assume 1) stable growth in GFA under management backed by on-track third-party GFA expansion offset diminishing Vanke delivery; 2) relatively stable property management fees with no signi cant fee increases in the near term, leading to a normalized margin trend; 3) in-line growth in <sup>fi</sup>the community VAS; 4) developer-related business (DVAS and AIoT) may still drag pro tability; and 4) less support from Vanke.

## BEAR CASE

## HK\$9.69

## 5x 2025E P/E

Zero GFA under management increase, no revenue contribution from developers, and normalized net margin: We assume 1) no new GFA from parent company or thirdparty expansion; 2) no PFM revenue from Vanke's projects; 3) zero contribution from cyclical and property-related CVAS; 4) zero VAS from developer revenue contribution; 5) zero contribution from AIoT business from developers; and 6) normalized net pro t margin.

## Risk Reward - Poly Property Servic<sub>Risk Reward – Poly Property Services Co Ltd (6049.HK)</sub>

Solid Growth with High Visibility

## HK\$32.41PRICE TARGET

We apply a 10x target P/E to our 2025 EPS estimate to arrive at our price target. Our target P/E multiple is derived from our industry scorecard, based on seven metrics: GFA growth pro<sup>fi</sup>le, parent developer, <sup>fi</sup>nancials and cash <sup>fl</sup>ows, revenue quality, service quality, pro<sup>fi</sup>tability, and incentive scheme. Poly Property Services has a diversi <sup>fi</sup>ed portfolio mix and rapid growth potential, in our view.

## OVERWEIGHT THESIS

▪ We still view the company’s fundamentals as solid, and its unrivaled edge in public
service as a strength in third-party expansion competition.
▪ Parent developer with SOE background with no liquidity issues.
▪ Attractive ex-cash valuation of 1.6x 2024E P/E, and potential dividend upside.
▪ We see high earnings visibility in medium term, with c.8% earnings CAGR projected for the next few years.

![](images/407e9082ac7670396216d15c9b10ae2c03c1a05813d45a686093f7c05f753bb4.jpg)

Secular Growth: Positive View descriptions of Risk Rewards Themes here

## BULL CASE

## 15x 2025E P/E

## HK\$48.62

Accelerating GFA growth with better-thanexpected margin: Higher-than-expected growth in GFA under management, driven by faster-than-expected third-party GFA expansion. Higher-than-expected property management fee increases as property owners value high-quality service more, which ultimately leads to faster-thanexpected margin expansion. Increasing interest in the industry from more IPOs and rising social and government attention, driving valuation upward.

## BASE CASE

## 10x 2025E P/E

## HK\$32.41

Stable GFA growth with steady ASP and normalized margin: Stable growth in GFA under management, backed by steady support from Poly Developments, and ontrack third-party GFA expansion. We expect relatively stable property management fees with no signi cant increases in the near term.

## BEAR CASE

## HK\$16.21

## 5x 2025E P/E

Slower-than-expected GFA growth with margin squeeze: Slower-than-expected growth in GFA under management, hampered by a slower delivery schedule from Poly Developments, along with weaker-than-expected third-party GFA expansion. Tightening regulation on management fees and slower-than-expected VAS development further cap earnings upside.

Failure to raise property management fees, resulting in margin squeeze due to rising labor costs.

Our target P/E multiple is derived from our industry scorecard, based on seven metrics: GFA growth pro le, parent developer, nancials and cash ows, revenue quality, service quality, pro <sup>fi</sup>tability, and incentive scheme. The higher a company’s score, the higher the target multiple that we assign to the company.

## EQUAL-WEIGHT THESIS

▪ Company has high-quality portfolio of GFA under management with improving thirdparty expansion capability and large exposure to tier 1 and 2 cities. ▪ DVAS segment may further shrink amid weak property sales. ▪ Company needs time to regain growth momentum and prove organic growth ability after digesting parentco's liquidity risk.

## Risk Reward Themes

Secular Growth: Positive View descriptions of Risk Rewards Themes here

## BULL CASE

## 11x 2025E P/E

HK\$2.41

Accelerating GFA growth with better-thanexpected margin: Higher-than-expected GFA under management growth, driven by fasterthan-expected third-party GFA expansion. Higher-than-expected property management fee increase, as property owners are willing to pay more for high service quality, which ultimately leads to faster-than-expected margin expansion. Increasing interest in the industry, as a consequence of more IPOs and rising government attention, driving valuation upward.

## BASE CASE

## 7x 2025E P/E

## HK\$1.53

Stable GFA growth with steady ASP and normalized margin: Stable growth in GFA under management, backed by on-track third-party GFA expansion. We expect relatively stable property management fees with no signi cant increases in the near term.

## BEAR CASE

## 3x 2025E P/E

HK\$0.66

Slower-than-expected GFA growth with margin squeeze: Slower-than expected GFA under management growth, dragged down by slower delivery schedule from Sunac and weaker-than-expected third-party GFA expansion. Tightening regulation on management fees and slower-than-expected VAS development further cap earnings upside.

Higher margin with less industry competition.

## RISKS TO DOWNSIDE

Weaker property market with further liquidity risks weighing on investor sentiment.

Slower-than-expected development cycle amid macro headwinds weighing on revenue booking.

Lower-than-expected margin with intensifying industry competition.

## Risk Reward - China World Trade Cent<sub>Risk Reward – China World Trade Center Co., Ltd (600007.SS)</sub>

(600007.SS)Beijing's High-end Commercial Play

Rmb25.91PRICE TARGET Based on 20x 2024E P/E. Our target multiple is based on A-share peers.

## RISK REWARD CHART

![](images/ee9e44a1204221ecbbdd6519a0d34db0d247c316d7e96b16b8cb8861a37a2022.jpg)
Key: Historical Stock Performance Current Stock Price Price Target
Source: Re nitiv, Morgan Stanley Research

## OVERWEIGHT THESIS

▪ CWTC holds a scarce high-end commercial project in a core Beijing location ▪ Resilient earnings growth with a 3% CAGR in 2023-26 supported by retail sales growth, hotel recovery and the ramp-up of apartments after reopening in 2020 ▪ CWTC has a cash cow business with 4% dividend yield and 8% FCF yield

![](images/40f2370311ecc88daad5b0ec9506fb2567eab7924f78316e85fb7729b78403a5.jpg)

## Risk Reward Themes

Secular Growth: Positive View descriptions of Risk Rewards Themes here

## BULL CASE

## Rmb32.39

## 25x 2024E P/E

Stronger-than-expected consumption recovery and retail sales growth in both luxury and non-luxury shopping malls.

Stronger-than-expected macro economy with higher employment rate, leading to higher of ce rental and occupancy rate.

Stronger-than-expected rental demand drives up apartment occupancy rate and rental.

More project expansion plans and thirdparty asset light expansion plans

## BASE CASE

## Rmb25.91

## 20x 2024E P/E

Moderate consumption recovery, leading to retail sales growth of -5%/-5%/10% in 2024/25/26E.

Stable macro economy recovery, leading to stable recovery of of ce occupancy rate and stable growth of of ce rental.

Stable rental and travel demand, leading to stable recovery of apartments and hotels.

## BEAR CASE

## Rmb12.95

## 10x 2024E P/E

Slower-than-expected consumption recovery and increasing luxury outbound consumption.

Slower-than-expected macro recovery with lower employment, leading to lower of ce rental and occupancy rate.

Slower-than-expected macro recovery leading to lower revenue from hotels and apartments.

![](images/59a6ca5989832a7872cfd018d52df0deac8fd930c3a796e7771fcda8fa2e0bd5.jpg)

## RISKS TO UPSIDE

Potential third-party asset-light shopping mall operation business

Higher than expected consumption recovery

Higher than expected dividend payout

## RISKS TO DOWNSIDE

Highly concentrated portfolio exposed to Beijing market risk

Softening luxury sales may weigh on CWTC's retail sales in the short term

Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold, and sell but represent recommended relative weightings (see definitions below). To satisfy regulatory requirements, we correspond Overweight, our most positive stock rating, with a buy recommendation; we correspond Equal-weight and Not-Rated to hold and Underweight to sell recommendations, respectively.

## Analyst Stock Ratings

Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.

Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months

Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.

Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.

Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.

## Analyst Industry Views

Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below. Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below. Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.
